\lxSVG@closescope {\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}\hss}\lxSVG@discardpath\lxSVG@closescope \hss}}\lxSVG@closescope\endpgfpicture}}}\,=-q-q^{-1}\Bigr)}

(88)

The skein algebra
\Sk(C)
associated to an oriented closed surface
C
is defined as

\Sk(C):=\Sk(C\times[0,1],\SU(2))~~,

(89)

where the multiplication
\Sk(C)\times\Sk(C)\to\Sk(C)
is given by stacking. As a result,
\Sk(C)
is a
\mathbb{C}[q^{\pm\frac{1}{2}}]
-associative algebra
Tur 91
.

At the
q=1
specialization, the skein module
\Sk(M_{3})
becomes a commutative algebra. Moreover, it was shown in
Bul 97
;
PS 00
that by assigning a loop
\gamma:S^{1}\to M_{3}
to
\Tr(\rho(\gamma))
where
\rho:\pi_{1}(M_{3})\to\SL(2,\mathbb{C})
is the holonomy homomorphism, the classical limit
q=1
of
\Sk(M_{3})
is isomorphic to the coordinate ring of the character variety
\mathcal{M}_{\text{flat}}(M_{3},\SL(2,\mathbb{C}))
.
Hence, the skein module
\Sk(M_{3})
can be understood as a BV quantization
GJS19a

\Sk(M_{3})\cong\textrm{BV}^{q}(\mathcal{M}_{\text{flat}}(M_{3},\SL(2,\mathbb{C})))~.

The skein module of a closed 3-manifold will be studied in §
3.2
.

If a 3-manifold has a boundary
\partial M_{3}=C
, then we have a module
\Sk(C)

\circlearrowright

\Sk(M_{3})
by pushing a framed links in a thickened boundary
C\times[0,1]
into the bulk
M_{3}
.
In fact,
\mathcal{M}_{\text{flat}}(M_{3},\SL(2,\mathbb{C}))
is a holomorphic Lagrangian submanifold of
\mathcal{M}_{\text{flat}}(C,\SL(2,\mathbb{C}))
with respect to the holomorphic symplectic form
\Omega_{J}
. Therefore, it can be understood as an
(A,B,A)
-brane
\mathfrak{B}_{H}
on
\mathcal{M}_{\text{flat}}(C,\SL(2,\mathbb{C}))
, called a
Heegaard brane
. From the viewpoint of brane quantization, the action of the skein algebra can be understood as

\begin{array}[]{ccc}\Sk(C)&\cong&\Hom(\mathfrak{B}_{\text{cc}},\mathfrak{B}_{\text{cc}})\\
\rotatebox[origin]{180.0}{$\circlearrowright$}&&\rotatebox[origin]{180.0}{$\circlearrowright$}\\
\Sk(M_{3})&\cong&\Hom(\mathfrak{B}_{\text{cc}},\mathfrak{B}_{H})\\
\end{array}~.

(90)

Of our particular interest is the skein algebra
\Sk(T^{2})
of a torus, which is the
t=q
specialization of
S\!\accentset{\mbox{\large.\hskip-1.33333pt.}}{H}

BP 00
. Also, the skein module
\Sk(S^{1}\times D^{2})
of the solid torus is the Grothendieck ring of the category of finite-dimensional representations of
U_{q}(\mathfrak{sl}(2))

\Sk(S^{1}\times D^{2})\cong K^{0}(\mathsf{Rep}\,U_{q}(\mathfrak{sl}(2)))\otimes\mathbb{C}[q^{\pm\frac{1}{2}}]~~,

(91)

which is spanned by Chebyshev polynomials
S_{j}(z)
of the second kind
FG 00
. They are recursively defined by

zS_{j}(z)=S_{j+1}(z)+S_{j-1}(z)

(92)

with the initial conditions
S_{0}(z)=1,S_{1}(z)=z
, and they are actually the
t=q
specialization of the Macdonald polynomials

S_{j}(X+X^{-1})=P_{j}(X;q,t=q)=\frac{X^{j+1}-X^{-j-1}}{X-X^{-1}}~.

(93)

Consequently, the polynomial representation
\mathscr{P}
of
S\!\accentset{\mbox{\large.\hskip-1.33333pt.}}{H}_{t=q}
is indeed the skein module
\Sk(T^{2})

\circlearrowright

\Sk(S^{1}\times D^{2})
. In fact, the support of the Heegaard brane for the solid torus is given by
y=2
, which is the
A
-polynomial of the unknot complement in
S^{3}
. Indeed the eigenvalue of the
y
operator on
S_{j}(X+X^{-1})
under the polynomial representation
\mathscr{P}
at
t=q
is
q^{j+1}+q^{-j-1}
and its classical limit is
y=2
.
Thus, the polynomial representation
\mathscr{P}
of
S\!\accentset{\mbox{\large.\hskip-1.33333pt.}}{H}
can be understood as the
t
-deformation of the skein module
\Sk(T^{2})

\circlearrowright

\Sk(S^{1}\times D^{2})

Hik 19
.

Let us consider how the symmetries of
S\!\accentset{\mbox{\large.\hskip-1.33333pt.}}{H}
act on the polynomial representation
\mathscr{P}
. For instance, an action of
\PSL(2,\mathbb{Z})
on
\mathscr{P}
can be seen by using the maps (
57
) combined with (
80
). It is easy to see from (
57
) that the generators of
\PSL(2,\mathbb{Z})
maps
\mathfrak{B}_{\mathbf{P}}
to another
(A,B,A)
brane

\displaystyle\tau_{+}(\mathbf{P})

\displaystyle=\{xy-z=\tilde{t}+\tilde{t}^{-1}~,~y=\tilde{t}^{-1}x\}~,

(94)

\displaystyle\tau_{-}(\mathbf{P})

\displaystyle=\{y=\tilde{t}+\tilde{t}^{-1}~,~z=\tilde{t}^{-1}x\}~,

\displaystyle\sigma(\mathbf{P})

\displaystyle=\{x=\tilde{t}+\tilde{t}^{-1}~,~y=\tilde{t}^{-1}z\}~.

Under the modular
T
-transformation
\tau_{-}
, the support does not change and the polynomial representation
\mathscr{P}
is invariant,
\tau_{-}(\mathscr{P})\cong\mathscr{P}
since the Macdonald polynomials are transformed under the modular
T
-transformation
\tau_{-}
as

\tau_{-}(P_{j})=q^{-\frac{j^{2}}{2}}t^{-j}P_{j}\quad\rightsquigarrow\quad T_{jj^{\prime}}=q^{-\frac{j^{2}}{2}}t^{-j}\delta_{jj^{\prime}}~.

(95)

The proof is given at the end (
244
) of Appendix
B.2.1
.
The image
\sigma(\mathscr{P})
of the polynomial representation of
S\!\accentset{\mbox{\large.\hskip-1.33333pt.}}{H}
under the
S
-transformation
\sigma
is called the
functional representation
, which is explained in Appendix
B.2.2
. As for the group
\Xi
of the sign changes, the image
\xi_{1}(\mathscr{P})
is isomorphic to itself
\mathscr{P}\cong\xi_{1}(\mathscr{P})
. On the other hand, the image under the involution
\xi_{2}
can be obtained by multiplying the minus sign to
y
and
z
as in (
33
) and the support of the corresponding brane is

\xi_{2}(\mathbf{P})=\{y=-\tilde{t}-\tilde{t}^{-1}~,~z=-\tilde{t}^{-1}x\}~.

(96)

Finally, the outer automorphism (
59
) changes the Chan-Paton bundle of
\mathfrak{B}_{\mathbf{P}}
as explained in §
2.3.1
and the support becomes

\iota(\mathbf{P})=\{y=\tilde{t}+\tilde{t}^{-1}~,~z=\tilde{t}x\}~.

(97)

Note that the
\iota
image
\iota(\mathscr{P})
of the polynomial representation can be obtained by changing
t\to q/t
in (
80
).

The perspective from the brane quantization also sheds new light on infinite-dimensional representations. We have seen that Cherednik’s polynomial representation (
80
) corresponds to the
A
-brane
\mathfrak{B}_{\mathbf{P}}
(
87
) at the particular value of
y
. It is natural to expect that it can be deformed in such a way that the corresponding brane is supported on a generic point of
y
.

This consideration leads us to the following.
Let us consider the multiplicative system
\widetilde{M}\subset\mathbb{C}_{q,t}[X^{\pm}]
generated by all elements of the form
(q^{\ell}X-q^{-\ell}X^{-1})
for all integers
\ell\in\mathbb{Z}
. Then there is a family of representations of
S\!\accentset{\mbox{\large.\hskip-1.33333pt.}}{H}
on the localization
8
8

8

In other words,
\mathscr{P}^{y_{1}}
is the ring of rational functions with coefficients in
\mathbb{C}_{q,t}
where denominators are always elements in the multiplicative system
\widetilde{M}
such as

\frac{f(X)}{(q^{-m}X-q^{m}X^{-1})^{k_{-m}}\cdots(X-X^{-1})^{k_{0}}\cdots(q^{\ell}X-q^{-\ell}X^{-1})^{k_{\ell}}}~,\qquad f(X)\in\mathbb{C}_{q,t}[X^{\pm}]~.

of the ring of Laurent polynomials by
\widetilde{M}

\mathscr{P}^{y_{1}}=\widetilde{M}^{-1}\mathbb{C}_{q,t}[X^{\pm}]~,

(98)

labeled by a parameter
y_{1}\in\mathbb{C}^{\times}
where the representations are defined by

\displaystyle x

\displaystyle\mapsto X+X^{-1},

(99)

\displaystyle\pol_{y_{1}}:S\!\accentset{\mbox{\large.\hskip-1.33333pt.}}{H}\to\End(\mathscr{P}^{y_{1}}),\quad y

\displaystyle\mapsto y_{1}\frac{tX-t^{-1}X^{-1}}{X-X^{-1}}\varpi+y_{1}^{-1}\frac{t^{-1}X-tX^{-1}}{X-X^{-1}}\varpi^{-1},

\displaystyle z

\displaystyle\mapsto q^{\frac{1}{2}}y_{1}X\frac{tX-t^{-1}X^{-1}}{X-X^{-1}}\varpi+q^{\frac{1}{2}}(y_{1}X)^{-1}\frac{t^{-1}X-tX^{-1}}{X-X^{-1}}\varpi^{-1}~.

Concretely, one is free to deform Cherednik’s polynomial representation (
80
) to this larger representation parametrized by
y_{1}
, as long as we allow denominators to be elements of the multiplicative system
\widetilde{M}
.
Only at
y_{1}=1
, it decomposes into two irreducible representations where one is Cherednik’s polynomial representation, and the other irreducible representation is

\widetilde{M}^{-1}\mathbb{C}_{q,t}[X^{\pm}]^{\mathbb{Z}_{2}}~.

When
t=1
, the story reduces to the polynomial representations of the symmetrized quantum torus discussed in Appendix
C.3.2
. Thus, the support of the corresponding brane
\mathfrak{B}_{\mathbf{P}}^{y_{1}}
is expected to be

\mathrm{supp}~\mathfrak{B}_{\mathbf{P}}^{y_{1}}=\{y=y_{1}^{-1}\tilde{t}+y_{1}\tilde{t}^{-1}\}~.

In fact, the eigenfunction of
y
under
\pol_{y_{1}}
that generalizes the Macdonald polynomials is constructed in
BKK 15
;
KZ 21
;
Kor 18

9
9

9

\mathcal{Z}
is the so-called uncapped vertex function in the quantum K-theory of
T^{*}\mathbb{C}\mathbf{P}^{1}
.

\mathcal{Z}(X,y_{1},q,t)={}_{2}\phi_{1}\left(y_{1}^{2},t^{2};q^{2}t^{-2}y_{1}^{2};q^{2};q^{2}t^{-2}X^{-2}\right)\,,~

(100)

where the eigenvalue is

\pol_{y_{1}}(y)\cdot\mathcal{Z}=(y_{1}^{-1}t+y_{1}t^{-1})\mathcal{Z}\,.

(101)

Thus, for a generic value of
y_{1}
, the eigenfunction is an infinite hypergeometric series (
100
). However, as easily seen from (
81
),
the series truncates to the symmetric Macdonald polynomial

\mathcal{Z}(X,y_{1}=q^{-j},q,t)=X^{-j}P_{j}(X;q,t)\,.

(102)

at the specialization
y_{1}=q^{-j}

(j\in\mathbb{Z}_{\geq 0})
.

A geometric interpretation of the multiplicative system
\widetilde{M}
can be given by thinking about the
t=1
limit, where we are interested in the quotient map
\mathbb{C}^{\times}\times\mathbb{C}^{\times}\rightarrow(\mathbb{C}^{\times}\times\mathbb{C}^{\times})/\mathbb{Z}_{2}
. After deforming the target of this covering map, no natural ramified twofold cover by
\mathbb{C}^{\times}\times\mathbb{C}^{\times}
exists. However, such a cover can be constructed once we extract the
\mathbb{Z}_{2}
-invariant points
X=\pm 1
. In fact,
\mathscr{O}(\mathbb{C}^{\times}\backslash\{X=\pm 1\})
admits the generator
\frac{1}{X-X^{-1}}
. A related story exists in the rational limit, where the relevant geometry is the deformation of the
A_{1}
singularity
(\mathbb{C}\times\mathbb{C})/\mathbb{Z}_{2}
to the total space of
T^{*}\mathbb{C}P^{1}
; we discuss this in detail in Appendix
B.2.4
.

2.6
Branes with compact supports and finite-dimensional representations:

object matching

Cherednik’s polynomial representation is of particular significance due to the theorems of Cherednik
(
Che 05
, §2.8–9)
, which classify finite-dimensional representations of
S\!\accentset{\mbox{\large.\hskip-1.33333pt.}}{H}
obtained as quotients of the polynomial representation paired with the action of outer automorphisms. Similar to the theory of Verma modules, the polynomial representation is generically irreducible. A raising operator (
85
) never be null since the Macdonald polynomial
P_{2j}
always has a factor
(1-q^{2j}t^{2})
in the denominator. However, it can occur that a lowering operator
\mathsf{L}_{j}
annihilates one of the Macdonald polynomials
P_{j}
when certain conditions on the central character are satisfied. If this occurs,
P_{j}
generates a subrepresentation, and a finite-dimensional representation of the spherical DAHA appears as the quotient
\mathscr{P}/(P_{j})
.
We can therefore study finite-dimensional representations by asking that the condition
\pol(\mathsf{L}_{j})\cdot P_{j}=0
be satisfied for some
j
,
i.e.
that the factor

\frac{(1-q^{2j})(1-q^{(j-1)}t^{2})(1+q^{(j-1)}t^{2})}{q^{2j}t^{2}(q^{2(j-1)}t^{2}-1)}

(103)

on the right hand side of (
86
) vanishes.

This amounts to the following three cases:

\displaystyle q^{2n}

\displaystyle=1~,

(104a)

\displaystyle t^{2}

\displaystyle=-q^{-k}~,

(104b)

\displaystyle t^{2}

\displaystyle=q^{-(2\ell-1)}~.

(104c)

Here, the exponent in the right hand side of (
104c
) must be an odd integer in order for the denominators of Macdonald polynomials as well as (
103
) to be non-zero; even exponents are excluded by the definition of the coefficient ring
\mathbb{C}_{q,t}
in (
45
). We write this odd integer as
2\ell-1
.
Each of these separate shortening conditions will naturally appear as an existence condition of an
A
-brane with compact support in what follows; we will examine each of the resulting finite-dimensional representations and the corresponding compact Lagrangian branes in turn.

2.6.1
Generic fibers of the Hitchin fibration

First we consider analogous
A
-branes in this setting; the ones supported on generic fibers in the Hitchin fibration. As explained in §
2.1
, the Hitchin fibration (
19
) is completely integrable, and a generic Hitchin fiber
\mathbf{F}
is holomorphic in complex structure
I
while it is a complex Lagrangian submanifold from the viewpoint of the holomorphic two-form
\Omega_{I}
for a generic ramification data (
8
). Namely, it is a Lagrangian submanifold of type
(B,A,A)
for any values of
(\alpha_{p},\beta_{p},\gamma_{p})
-triple. Therefore, a generic fiber
\mathbf{F}
can be Lagrangian in a symplectic manifold
(\mathfrak{X},\omega_{\mathfrak{X}})
only when the canonical coisotropic brane
\mathfrak{B}_{\text{cc}}
obeys the condition
\theta=0
in (
64
) so that

\omega_{\mathfrak{X}}=-\frac{\omega_{K}}{\hbar}~,\quad\textrm{and}\quad F+B=\frac{\omega_{I}}{\hbar}~.

(105)

With
\theta\neq 0
, there is no
A
-brane supported on
\mathbf{F}
in the symplectic manifold
(\mathfrak{X},\omega_{\mathfrak{X}})
. Accordingly,
\hbar=|\hbar|
is real (
i.e.

|q|=1
), and the canonical coisotropic brane
\mathfrak{B}_{\text{cc}}
is an
A
-brane of type
(B,A,A)
.

An analogous brane appears in the brane quantization of
\mathbb{C}^{\times}\times\mathbb{C}^{\times}
for the quantum torus algebra. As in Appendix
C.2.1
, a brane is supported on a fiber
T^{2}
of the elliptic fibration
T^{*}T^{2}\cong\mathbb{C}^{\times}\times\mathbb{C}^{\times}
, which gives rise to a finite-dimensional representation, called the cyclic representation. Therefore, we can study a brane supported on a generic fiber
\mathbf{F}
of the Hitchin fibration, comparing with the case of the quantum torus algebra.

The branes are indexed by a position of the Hitchin base
\mathcal{B}_{H}
(see also Appendix
C.2.1
). Also, the flatness condition (
75
) of the line bundle
\mathcal{L}^{\prime}
an
A
-brane supported
\mathfrak{B}_{\mathbf{F}}
is

F^{\prime}_{\mathbf{F}}+B\big|_{\mathbf{F}}=0~.

Since
\mathbf{F}
is topologically a two-torus, the flat
\Spin^{c}
structure
\mathcal{L}^{\prime}\otimes K_{\mathbf{L}}^{-1/2}
of
\mathfrak{B}_{\mathbf{F}}
can have non-trivial
\U(1)^{2}
holonomy with a choice of spin structure
GW 09
.
The branes
\mathfrak{B}_{\mathbf{F}}^{\lambda}
are parametrized by
\lambda=(x_{m},y_{m})\in\mathbb{C}^{\times}\times\mathbb{C}^{\times}
where the absolute values
(|x_{m}|,|y_{m}|)
describe its position and the angular phases illustrate the
\U(1)^{2}
holonomy with a choice of spin structures. Namely, the angular phase
\U(1)
encodes the holonomy
\U(1)
and a choice of spin structures
\mathbb{Z}_{2}
along a one-cycle of a Riemann surface via

1\to\mathbb{Z}_{2}\to\U(1)\to\U(1)\to 1~.

We assign the plus sign
+
for
1\in\mathbb{Z}_{2}
to the Ramond spin structure, and the minus sign
-
for
-1\in\mathbb{Z}_{2}
to the Neveu-Schwarz spin structure. The choice of spin structures appears in the representation of the symmetrized quantum torus discussed in Appendix
C.3.2
.

Consequently, the computation of the dimension (
78
) of the space
\Hom(\mathfrak{B}_{\text{cc}},\mathfrak{B}_{\mathbf{F}}^{\lambda})
is reduced to the period integral (
27
)

\displaystyle\dim\Hom(\mathfrak{B}_{\text{cc}},\mathfrak{B}_{\mathbf{F}}^{\lambda})=\int_{\mathbf{F}}\frac{F+B}{2\pi}=\int_{\mathbf{F}}\frac{\omega_{I}}{2\pi\hbar}=\frac{1}{\hbar}

(106)

for arbitrary
\lambda
. Hence, this leads to the Bohr-Sommerfeld quantization condition
\hbar=1/m
, or equivalently that
q=e^{2\pi i/m}
is a primitive
m
-th root of unity for
m\in\mathbb{Z}_{>0}
. In fact, since
[F/2\pi]
is an integral cohomology class in
H^{2}(\mathfrak{X},\mathbb{Z})
, the fiber class relation (
21
) requires
\int_{\mathbf{F}}F/2\pi
to be an even integer. Thus, if
m
is an odd positive integer, then we need non-trivial
B
-flux with

\int_{\mathbf{F}}\frac{B}{2\pi}=-\int_{\mathbf{F}}\frac{F^{\prime}_{\mathbf{F}}}{2\pi}=1~,

(107)

up to an even integer shift. For instance, this can be achieved if the
B
-field flux over
\mathbf{V}
is
1/2
and those over the exceptional divisors
\mathbf{D}_{i}
(
i=1,\ldots,4
) are zero.

In order for the
(\mathfrak{B}_{\text{cc}},\mathfrak{B}_{\mathbf{F}}^{\lambda})
-strings exist,
q
has to be a primitive
m
-th root of unity whereas
t
can be generic. Under this condition, the action of
S\!\accentset{\mbox{\large.\hskip-1.33333pt.}}{H}
under the generalized polynomial representation
\pol_{y_{1}}
in (
99
) commute with
X^{m}-x_{m}
for
x_{m}\in\mathbb{C}^{\times}
because the shift operator
\varpi
acts trivially on it. Consequently, the ideal
(X^{m}-x_{m})
is invariant under
\pol_{y_{1}}
so that the quotient space

\mathscr{F}^{\lambda}_{m}=\mathscr{P}^{y_{1}}/(X^{m}-x_{m})~,

is also a representation of
S\!\accentset{\mbox{\large.\hskip-1.33333pt.}}{H}
. Since the Taylor expansion of a denominator in the multiplicative system
\widetilde{M}
always truncates under the condition
X^{m}=x_{m}
, this is indeed an
m
-dimensional representation parametrized by
\lambda=(x_{m},y_{m})
where
y_{1}
is any
m
-th root of
y_{m}
. Hence, we can identify
\Hom(\mathfrak{B}_{\text{cc}},\mathfrak{B}_{\mathbf{F}}^{\lambda})
with
\mathscr{F}^{\lambda}_{m}
when
q
is a primitive
m
-th root of unity where the parameter
\lambda\in\mathbb{C}^{\times}\times\mathbb{C}^{\times}
exactly matches.

For generic values of
\lambda=(x_{m},y_{m})
, the support of a brane
\mathfrak{B}_{\mathbf{F}}^{\lambda}
is mapped to another Hitchin fiber up to Hamiltonian isotopy under the
\PSL(2,\mathbb{Z})
action, and the holonomy of the Chan-Paton bundle, which is a point in the dual torus
\Jac(\mathbf{F})
, is also transformed appropriately. Namely,
\PSL(2,\mathbb{Z})
acts on
\lambda
. On the other hand, a generic fiber is invariant as a set under the group
\Xi
of the sign changes as we have seen in §
2.1
. Correspondingly, the representation
\mathscr{F}^{\lambda}_{m}
is invariant under
\Xi
at a generic value of
\lambda
.

Setting
y_{1}=1
, we can symmetrize the story
(
Che 05
, Thm 2.8.5 (iv))
. Namely, since the ideal
(X^{m}+X^{-m}-x_{m}-x_{m}^{-1})
is invariant under Cherednik’s polynomial representation
\mathscr{P}
due to the same reason, we have an
m
-dimensional representation

\mathscr{F}^{(x_{m},+)}_{m}=\mathscr{P}/(X^{m}+X^{-m}-x_{m}-x_{m}^{-1})~.

(108)

In this case, the corresponding brane
\mathfrak{B}_{\mathbf{F}}^{(x_{m},+)}
supported on a Hitchin fiber intersects with the support
\mathbf{P}
(
87
) of the polynomial representation. Also, the Chan-Paton bundle has the trivial holonomy and the Ramond spin structure around one generator, say the
(0,1)
-cycle, of
\pi_{1}(\mathbf{F})\cong\mathbb{Z}\oplus\mathbb{Z}
. The parameter
x_{m}
encodes its position in the
x
coordinate and the holonomy around the other generator of
\pi_{1}(\mathbf{F})
.

Therefore, the representations of this family are analogous to the finite-dimensional representations of both symmetrized and ordinary quantum torus in terms of
A
-branes on fibers of the elliptic fibration of the target in the 2d
A
-models as illustrated in Appendix
C
.
As in the case of the symmetrized quantum torus Appendix
C.3
, if a brane
\mathfrak{B}_{\mathbf{F}}
with trivial holonomies moves to a special position, we will see below that a special phenomenon occurs.

2.6.2
Irreducible components in singular fibers of type
I_{2}

As in Figure
2
, the Hitchin fibration has three singular fibers of Kodaira type
I_{2}
for generic ramification parameters of
(\alpha_{p},\beta_{p},\gamma_{p})
. Since they are still fibers in the Hitchin fibration, the irreducible components
\mathbf{U}_{i}
(
i=1,\ldots,6
) in a singular fiber are also Lagrangian submanifolds of type
(B,A,A)
. Therefore,
\mathfrak{B}_{\text{cc}}
needs to satisfy (
105
) in order for
\mathfrak{B}_{\mathbf{U}_{i}}
to be
A
-branes as in the previous subsection.

For instance, let us investigate a module that the brane
\mathfrak{B}_{\mathbf{U}_{1}}
gives rise to. The curvature of the line bundle
\mathcal{L}^{\prime}
should obey the flatness condition (
75
)

F^{\prime}_{\mathbf{U}_{1}}+\left.B\right|_{\mathbf{U}_{1}}=0~.

(109)

Since
\mathbf{U}_{1}
is topologically
\mathbb{C}\mathbf{P}^{1}
and a position is fixed, there is no deformation parameter associated to the brane
\mathfrak{B}_{\mathbf{U}_{1}}
.
Subsequently, one can evaluate the dimension formula (
78
)

\displaystyle\dim\Hom(\mathfrak{B}_{\text{cc}},\mathfrak{B}_{\mathbf{U}_{1}})=\int_{\mathbf{U}_{1}}\frac{F+B}{2\pi}=\int_{\mathbf{U}_{1}}\frac{\omega_{I}}{2\pi\hbar}=\frac{1}{2\hbar}

(110)

Consequently, the brane
\mathfrak{B}_{\mathbf{U}_{1}}
can exist only at
1/(2\hbar)=n\in\mathbb{Z}_{>0}
, or equivalently when
q
is a primitive
2n
-th root of unity.

This is exactly one (
104a
) of the shortening conditions, and under this condition a lowering operator (
86
) annihilates the Macdonald polynomial

\pol(\mathsf{L}_{n})\cdot P_{n}(X;q,t)=0\qquad\textrm{where}\qquad P_{n}(X;q,t)=X^{n}+X^{-n}~~.

(111)

Therefore, the quotient space

\mathscr{U}_{n}^{(1)}:=\mathscr{P}/(P_{n})

(112)

by an ideal
(P_{n})
is an
n
-dimensional irreducible representation of spherical DAHA
(
Che 05
, Thm 2.8.5 (ii))
so that one can identify

\mathscr{U}_{n}^{(1)}=\Hom(\mathfrak{B}_{\text{cc}},\mathfrak{B}_{\mathbf{U}_{1}})~.

As seen in §
2.1
, the irreducible component
\mathbf{U}_{1}
is invariant under the sign change
\xi_{1}
whereas it is mapped to
\mathbf{U}_{2}
under
\xi_{2}
(
35
).
In fact, it follows from the form (
111
) of
P_{n}(X)
that the finite-dimensional module
\mathscr{U}_{n}^{(1)}
is invariant under the sign flip
\xi_{1}
.
On the other hand, the sign change
\xi_{2}
leads to another non-isomorphic finite-dimensional module. Thus, the brane
\xi_{2}(\mathfrak{B}_{\mathbf{U}_{1}})
corresponds to a brane supported on the other irreducible component
\mathbf{U}_{2}
in the same singular fiber from which the module comes from

\mathscr{U}_{n}^{(2)}:=\xi_{2}(\mathscr{U}_{n}^{(1)})=\Hom(\mathfrak{B}_{\text{cc}},\mathfrak{B}_{\mathbf{U}_{2}})~.

In a similar fashion, a brane
\mathfrak{B}_{\mathbf{U}_{i}}
supported on another irreducible component in a singular fiber gives rise to an image of
\mathscr{U}_{n}^{(1)}
under
\PSL(2,\mathbb{Z})
and the sign changes
\xi_{1,2}
. The transformation rule can be read off from (
42
) so that the branes
\mathfrak{B}_{\mathbf{U}_{1,2}}
are invariant under
\tau_{-}
whereas they are mapped as

\displaystyle\sigma(\mathfrak{B}_{\mathbf{U}_{1}})=\mathfrak{B}_{\mathbf{U}_{3}}~,

\displaystyle\sigma(\mathfrak{B}_{\mathbf{U}_{2}})=\mathfrak{B}_{\mathbf{U}_{4}}~,

(113)

\displaystyle\tau_{+}(\mathfrak{B}_{\mathbf{U}_{1}})=\mathfrak{B}_{\mathbf{U}_{5}}~,

\displaystyle\tau_{+}(\mathfrak{B}_{\mathbf{U}_{2}})=\mathfrak{B}_{\mathbf{U}_{6}}~.

The corresponding modules
\mathscr{U}_{n}^{(i)}
are obtained from
\mathscr{U}_{n}^{(1)}
in the same way.

2.6.3
Moduli space of
G
-bundles

Next, we consider a brane
\mathfrak{B}_{\mathbf{V}}
supported on the moduli space
\mathbf{V}
of
G
-bundles. For the sake of brevity, let us first see the case of
\beta_{p}=0
. If
\hbar
is real, only
\alpha_{p}
can be turned on while
\gamma_{p}
must vanish in order for
\mathbf{V}
to be Lagrangian with respect to
\omega_{K}
. As
\hbar=|\hbar|e^{i\theta}
is rotated
\theta\neq 0
in the complex plane, the symplectic form we are interested in is also rotated from
\omega_{K}
to
\omega_{\mathfrak{X}}
according to (
64
). However, this rotation can be actually compensated by switching on
\gamma_{p}
so that
\mathbf{V}
can stay Lagrangian with respect to
\omega_{\mathfrak{X}}
.
According to (
26
) and (
64
), the set
\mathbf{V}
is Lagrangian with respect to
\omega_{\mathfrak{X}}
when the following condition holds:

\Imaginary\,\frac{\big(\frac{1}{2}-\talpha_{p}\big)+i{}_{p}}{\hbar}\;=\;0

(114)

As a simple check, one can easily see from (
26
) and (
67
) that the integral of the symplectic form is zero

\displaystyle\int_{\mathbf{V}}\frac{\Imaginary\,\Omega}{2\pi}

\displaystyle=\int_{\mathbf{V}}\frac{\omega_{\mathfrak{X}}}{2\pi}=0~,

(115)

In addition, if
\beta_{p}=0
, the submanifold
\mathbf{V}
is also Lagrangian with respect to
\omega_{J}
. Namely, it is a complex Lagrangian submanifold with respect to a holomorphic two-form
\omega_{\mathfrak{X}}+i\omega_{J}
. When
\tbeta_{p}
is varied,
\mathbf{V}
stays as a Lagrangian submanifold with respect to
\omega_{\mathfrak{X}}
while they are no longer Lagrangian with respect to
\omega_{J}
. In fact, the variation of
\beta_{p}
does not change the holomorphic symplectic form
\Omega_{J}=\omega_{K}+i\omega_{I}
, and therefore keeps
\omega_{\mathfrak{X}}
fixed. In conclusion,
\mathbf{V}
can be Lagrangian with respect to
\omega_{\mathfrak{X}}
only when (
114
) holds. Since our concern is the
A
-model in the symplectic manifold
(\mathfrak{X},\omega_{\mathfrak{X}})
, the value of
\beta_{p}
can be arbitrary.
For generic
(\beta_{p},\gamma_{p})
,
\mathbf{V}
is no longer a Lagrangian of type
(B,A,A)
, and it is therefore not contained in a fiber of the Hitchin fibration.
Nonetheless, unlike a Hitchin fiber, we can consider the
A
-model in a generic symplectic form
\omega_{\mathfrak{X}}
in (
64
) where
\hbar
can take any complex value.

Under the condition (
114
) with a generic value of
\hbar
,
\mathbf{V}
is a unique compact Lagrangian submanifold, which is topologically
\mathbb{C}\mathbf{P}^{1}
. Hence, there is no deformation parameter for
\mathfrak{B}_{\mathbf{V}}
.
Consequently, we obtain the dimension of the space of
(\mathfrak{B}_{\text{cc}},\mathfrak{B}_{\mathbf{V}})
-strings from (
67
)

\dim\Hom(\mathfrak{B}_{\text{cc}},\mathfrak{B}_{\mathbf{V}})=\int_{\mathbf{V}}\frac{F+B}{2\pi}=\frac{1}{2\hbar}-\frac{{}_{p}+i\talpha_{p}}{i\hbar}=\frac{1}{2\hbar}+2c-1~.

(116)

The Bohr-Sommerfeld quantization condition imposes its dimension as a positive integer
1/2\hbar+2c-1=k+1\in\mathbb{Z}_{>0}
, or equivalently
t^{2}=-q^{k+2}
.

One can observe that this quantization condition is equivalent to the image of the shortening condition (
104b
) under the involution
\iota
.
In fact, under the shortening condition
t^{2}=-q^{k+2}
, the lowering operator in the
\iota
-image of the polynomial representation becomes an annihilation operator

\pol(\mathsf{L}_{k+1})\cdot P_{k+1}(X;q,t)\Big|_{t\to\frac{q}{t}}=0~.

Consequently, the quotient space by an ideal
(P_{k+1})

\iota(\mathscr{V}_{k+1}):=\iota(\mathscr{P})/(P_{k+1}(X;q,\tfrac{q}{t}))~

(117)

is a
(k+1)
-dimensional irreducible representation of
S\!\accentset{\mbox{\large.\hskip-1.33333pt.}}{H}

OS 09
. This representation is called the
additional series
in
(
Che 05
, §2.8.2)
, and we identify

\iota(\mathscr{V}_{k+1})=\Hom(\mathfrak{B}_{\text{cc}},\mathfrak{B}_{\mathbf{V}})~.

In fact, the support (
97
) of the brane
\iota(\mathfrak{B}_{\mathbf{P}})
intersects with
\mathbf{V}
at
t^{2}=-q^{k+2}
so that
\Hom(\iota(\mathfrak{B}_{\mathbf{P}}),\mathfrak{B}_{\mathbf{V}})\cong\mathbb{C}
becomes non-trivial. Hence,
\iota(\mathscr{V}_{k+1})
can be obtained as the quotient of
\iota(\mathscr{P})
as in (
117
).

As we have seen at the end of §
2.1
, the submanifold
\mathbf{V}
is geometrically invariant under the sign changes
\xi_{1,2}
so that we expect that the corresponding module
\mathscr{V}_{k+1}
is also endowed with the same property.
When
t^{2}=-q^{k+2}
, the Macdonald polynomials obey

P_{k+1}(-X;q,\tfrac{q}{t})=(-1)^{k}P_{k+1}(X;q,\tfrac{q}{t})~,

which implies that
\iota(\mathscr{V}_{k+1})
is indeed invariant under
\xi_{1}
. In addition, it is easy to check that the full set of
y
-eigenvalues (the
\iota
-image of (
82
)) of
\iota(\mathscr{V}_{k+1})
is also invariant under
\xi_{2}
.

What makes the space of
(\mathfrak{B}_{\text{cc}},\mathfrak{B}_{\mathbf{V}})
-strings even more interesting is that it also carries a
\mathrm{PSL}(2,\mathbb{Z})
action. Indeed, as also explained in §
2.1
, the submanifold
\mathbf{V}
is invariant under
\mathrm{PSL}(2,\mathbb{Z})
symmetry and, as a result, the module
\iota(\mathscr{V}_{k+1})
is a
\mathrm{PSL}(2,\mathbb{Z})
representation.

Of course, it is then natural to ask
which
representation it is, and in particular, what the corresponding
S
and
T
matrices are. To this end, it is more convenient to consider the space of
(\mathfrak{B}_{\text{cc}},\mathfrak{B}_{\mathbf{V}})
-strings in the target
\mathfrak{X}_{\tilde{t}^{-1}}
under (
36
) or (
29
). Then, the corresponding representation is given by

\mathscr{V}_{k+1}:=\mathscr{P}/(P_{k+1})

(118)

under the shortening condition (
104b
). Since the basis fo
\mathscr{V}_{k+1}
is spanned by the Macdonald polynomials
P_{j}(X)

(j=0,\ldots,k)
, the modular
T
-transformation
\tau_{-}
acts diagonally in this basis due to (
95
). Under the modular
S
transformation, this basis is transformed to
P_{j}(Y)
and the submanifold
\mathbf{V}
intersects with both the support (
87
) of the branes
\mathfrak{B}_{\mathbf{P}}
and that (
94
) of
\sigma(\mathfrak{B}_{\mathbf{P}})
.
Hence, the modular
S
-matrix can be written as

S_{jj^{\prime}}=\pol(P_{j}(Y^{-1}))\cdot P_{j^{\prime}}(X)\big|_{X=t^{-1}}=P_{j}(tq^{j^{\prime}};q,t)\,P_{j^{\prime}}(t^{-1};q,t)~.

(119)

This is first introduced by Cherednik
Che95b
as a symmetric bilinear pairing of Macdonald polynomials, which we also denote by
\textbf{[}P_{j},P_{j^{\prime}}\textbf{]}
as in (
229
). Moreover, it becomes of rank
(k+1)
when
t^{2}=-q^{-k}
, and it acts on
\mathscr{V}_{k+1}
. Therefore, we find explicit forms of the
S
and
T
matrices as follows, and we will also find a 3d interpretation of this
\PSL(2,\mathbb{Z})
representation in §
3.1.1
.

Conjecture 2.1
.

The space
\mathscr{V}_{k+1}
is a
(k+1)
-dimensional
\mathrm{PSL}(2,\mathbb{Z})
representation, with modular
S
and
T
matrices given by

\displaystyle T_{jj^{\prime}}\big|_{\mathscr{V}_{k+1}}

\displaystyle=e^{\frac{\pi ik}{12}}q^{-\frac{k(k-1)}{12}}~i^{-j}q^{\frac{j(k-j)}{2}}\delta_{jj^{\prime}}\qquad 0\leq j,j^{\prime}\leq k

(120)

\displaystyle S_{jj^{\prime}}\big|_{\mathscr{V}_{k+1}}

\displaystyle=a_{k}^{-1}g_{j}(q,t=iq^{-k/2})^{-1}P_{j}(iq^{j^{\prime}-k/2};q,t=iq^{-k/2})\,P_{j^{\prime}}(iq^{k/2};q,t=iq^{-k/2})~.

These matrices provide the
\mathrm{PSL}(2,\mathbb{Z})
representation for “refined Chern-Simons theory”.

Here we normalize the modular
S
-transformation (
119
) by the Macdonald norm of type
A_{1}
(See (
223
) for the definition)

g_{j}(q,t):=\frac{(q^{2j};q^{-2})_{j}(t^{4};q^{2})_{j}}{(q^{2j-2};q^{-2})_{j}(t^{2}q^{2};q^{2})_{j}}

(121)

and

a_{k}=\begin{cases}\sqrt{2}\prod_{i=0}^{\frac{k-3}{2}}(q^{\frac{1}{4}+\frac{i}{2}}+q^{-\frac{1}{4}-\frac{i}{2}})&\quad k:~\textrm{odd}\\
2\prod_{i=0}^{\frac{k-4}{2}}(q^{\frac{1}{2}+\frac{i}{2}}+q^{-\frac{1}{2}-\frac{i}{2}})&\quad k:~\textrm{even}\end{cases}

so that
S^{2}=1
. We also normalize the
T
-transformation (
95
) by
e^{\pi ik/12}q^{-k(k-1)/12}
so that
(ST)^{3}=1
.
For example, the first non-trivial case occurs at
k=1

T\big|_{\mathscr{V}_{2}}=e^{\pi i/12}\begin{pmatrix}1&0\\
0&-i\end{pmatrix}~,\qquad S\big|_{\mathscr{V}_{2}}=\frac{1}{\sqrt{2}}\begin{pmatrix}1&-i(q^{\frac{1}{2}}-q^{-\frac{1}{2}})\\
i(q^{\frac{1}{2}}-q^{-\frac{1}{2}})^{-1}&-1\end{pmatrix}~.

Next, we turn to less familiar and more interesting modular representation that arises from another Lagrangian
A
-brane in a similar fashion.

2.6.4
Exceptional divisors

Now let us consider an interesting
A
-brane
\mathfrak{B}_{\mathbf{D}_{i}}
supported on an exceptional divisor
\mathbf{D}_{i}
,
i=1,\ldots,4
. As we reviewed in the earlier part of this section, the ramification parameters
(\talpha_{p},\tbeta_{p},{}_{p})
play the role of resolution/deformation parameters for
\mathbf{D}_{i}
. In particular, when
\beta_{p}=0
and
\hbar
is real, only
\alpha_{p}
can be turned on while
\gamma_{p}
must vanish in order for
\mathbf{D}_{i}
to be Lagrangian with respect to
\omega_{K}
. As
\hbar=|\hbar|e^{i\theta}
is rotated
\theta\neq 0
in the complex plane, the exceptional divisors
\mathbf{D}_{i}
stay Lagrangian with respect to
\omega_{\mathfrak{X}}
if the deformation parameter
{}_{p}+i\talpha_{p}\in\mathbb{C}
in complex structure
J
is proportional to
i\hbar
, namely,

\Imaginary\frac{{}_{p}+i\talpha_{p}}{2i\hbar}=0~.

(122)

Here the value of
\tbeta_{p}
can be arbitrary as in the previous case.
It is easy to verify from (
28
) and (
67
) that

\int_{\mathbf{D}_{i}}\frac{\Imaginary\,\Omega}{2\pi}=\int_{\mathbf{D}_{i}}\frac{\omega_{\mathfrak{X}}}{2\pi}=0~.

The story goes as before.
The flatness condition (
75
) of the Chan-Paton bundle for the brane
\mathfrak{B}_{\mathbf{D}_{i}}
is

F^{\prime}_{\mathbf{D}_{i}}+B\big|_{\mathbf{D}_{i}}=0~,

Since it is topologically
\mathbb{C}\mathbf{P}^{1}
, there is no holonomy and no deformation parameter for
\mathfrak{B}_{\mathbf{D}_{i}}
. Subsequently, the dimension can be computed as

\displaystyle\dim\Hom(\mathfrak{B}_{\text{cc}},\mathfrak{B}_{\mathbf{D}_{i}})=\int_{\mathbf{D}_{i}}\frac{F+B}{2\pi}=-c+\frac{1}{2}~.

(123)

The Bohr-Sommerfeld quantization condition imposes its dimension as a positive integer
-c+1/2=\ell\in\mathbb{Z}_{>0}
, or equivalently
t^{2}=q^{-(2\ell-1)}
, which is (
104c
).

When
t=q^{-(2\ell-1)/2}
, the lowering operator annihilates the Macdonald polynomial

\pol(\mathsf{L}_{2\ell})\cdot P_{2\ell}(X;q,t)=0~.

(124)

Therefore, the quotient space

\mathscr{D}_{2\ell}:=\mathscr{P}/(P_{2\ell})

(125)

by an ideal
(P_{2\ell})
is a
2\ell
-dimensional representation of
S\!\accentset{\mbox{\large.\hskip-1.33333pt.}}{H}
. In fact, it is not irreducible, and decomposes into two irreducible representations

\mathscr{D}_{2\ell}=\mathscr{D}_{\ell}^{(1)}\oplus\mathscr{D}_{\ell}^{(2)}~.

(126)

Because
P_{j}
and
P_{2\ell-j-1}
have the same eigenvalue of the Macdonald difference operator (
82
)
when
t=q^{-(2\ell-1)/2}
, their combinations indeed form bases of
\mathscr{D}_{\ell}^{(1,2)}

\mathscr{D}_{\ell}^{(1)}=\bigoplus_{j=0}^{\ell-1}\mathbb{C}_{q,t}\Bigl[\frac{P_{j}(X)}{P_{j}(t^{-1})}+\frac{P_{2\ell-j-1}(X)}{P_{2\ell-j-1}(t^{-1})}\Bigr]~,\quad\mathscr{D}_{\ell}^{(2)}=\bigoplus_{j=0}^{\ell-1}\mathbb{C}_{q,t}\Bigl[\frac{P_{j}(X)}{P_{j}(t^{-1})}-\frac{P_{2\ell-j-1}(X)}{P_{2\ell-j-1}(t^{-1})}\Bigr]~.

(127)

Consequently, they are related by the sign change
\mathscr{D}_{\ell}^{(2)}=\xi_{1}(\mathscr{D}_{\ell}^{(1)})
. In fact, the support (
87
) of the brane
\mathfrak{B}_{\mathbf{P}}
intersects with
\mathbf{D}_{1,2}
at
t=q^{-(2\ell-1)/2}
so that
\mathscr{D}_{\ell}^{(1)}\oplus\mathscr{D}_{\ell}^{(2)}
can be obtained as the quotient of
\mathscr{P}
as in (
125
).

Even when
t=-q^{-(2\ell-1)/2}
, the shortening condition (
124
) holds, but the eigenvalues (
82
) of the
y
-operator have the opposite sign as in (
96
). Therefore, the corresponding irreducible representations can be obtained by the sign change
\xi_{2}
in (
33
) from
\mathscr{D}_{\ell}^{(1,2)}
.

As a result, for
t^{2}=q^{-(2\ell-1)}
, there are four irreducible finite-dimensional representations
(
Che 05
, Thm 2.8.1)
that are obtained from
\mathscr{D}_{\ell}^{(1)}
by the sign changes
\xi_{1,2}
.
This is analogous to the relationship among the exceptional divisors under the sign changes (
34
). Therefore, we identify these modules to the spaces of open
(\mathfrak{B}_{\text{cc}},\mathfrak{B}_{\mathbf{D}_{i}})
-strings as

\displaystyle\mathscr{D}_{\ell}^{(1)}=\Hom(\mathfrak{B}_{\text{cc}},\mathfrak{B}_{\mathbf{D}_{1}})~,

\displaystyle\mathscr{D}_{\ell}^{(2)}=\xi_{1}(\mathscr{D}_{\ell}^{(1)})=\Hom(\mathfrak{B}_{\text{cc}},\mathfrak{B}_{\mathbf{D}_{2}})~,

(128)

\displaystyle\mathscr{D}_{\ell}^{(3)}:=\xi_{2}(\mathscr{D}_{\ell}^{(1)})=\Hom(\mathfrak{B}_{\text{cc}},\mathfrak{B}_{\mathbf{D}_{3}})~,

\displaystyle\mathscr{D}_{\ell}^{(4)}:=\xi_{2}(\mathscr{D}_{\ell}^{(2)})=\xi_{3}(\mathscr{D}_{\ell}^{(1)})=\Hom(\mathfrak{B}_{\text{cc}},\mathfrak{B}_{\mathbf{D}_{4}})~.

The modules
\mathscr{D}^{(1,2)}_{\ell}
can be obtained as the quotient of the polynomial representation because the support (
87
) of
\mathfrak{B}_{\mathbf{P}}
intersects with
\mathbf{D}_{1}
and
\mathbf{D}_{2}
. On the other hand, its
\xi_{2}
-image (
97
) intersects with
\mathbf{D}_{3}
and
\mathbf{D}_{4}
. (See also Figure
7
.)

Under the
\PSL(2,\mathbb{Z})
action, the four irreducible representations are transformed as in (
41
). Namely, the modular
T
-transformation
\tau_{-}
exchanges
\mathscr{D}_{\ell}^{(3)}
and
\mathscr{D}_{\ell}^{(4)}
whereas
\mathscr{D}_{\ell}^{(1)}
and
\mathscr{D}_{\ell}^{(2)}
are invariant. Also, the modular
S
-transformation
\sigma
exchanges
\mathscr{D}_{\ell}^{(2)}
and
\mathscr{D}_{\ell}^{(3)}
whereas the modules
\mathscr{D}_{\ell}^{(1)}
and
\mathscr{D}_{\ell}^{(4)}
are invariant.

\displaystyle\tau_{+}

\displaystyle:\mathscr{D}_{\ell}^{(2)}\leftrightarrow\mathscr{D}_{\ell}^{(4)}\quad\textrm{and}\quad\mathscr{D}_{\ell}^{(1)},~\mathscr{D}_{\ell}^{(3)}\quad\textrm{are invariant}~,

(129)

\displaystyle\tau_{-}

\displaystyle:\mathscr{D}_{\ell}^{(3)}\leftrightarrow\mathscr{D}_{\ell}^{(4)}\quad\textrm{and}\quad\mathscr{D}_{\ell}^{(1)},~\mathscr{D}_{\ell}^{(2)}\quad\textrm{are invariant}~,

\displaystyle\sigma

\displaystyle:\mathscr{D}_{\ell}^{(2)}\leftrightarrow\mathscr{D}_{\ell}^{(3)}\quad\textrm{and}\quad\mathscr{D}_{\ell}^{(1)},~\mathscr{D}_{\ell}^{(4)}\quad\textrm{are invariant}~.

Thus, only the module
\mathscr{D}_{\ell}^{(1)}=\Hom(\mathfrak{B}_{\text{cc}},\mathfrak{B}_{\mathbf{D}_{1}})
among the four modules becomes a
\PSL(2,\mathbb{Z})
representation.

Let us find the modular
S
and
T
matrices for this
\PSL(2,\mathbb{Z})
representation.
As we have seen, the polynomial representation
\mathscr{P}
captures both
\mathscr{D}_{\ell}^{(1)}
and
\mathscr{D}_{\ell}^{(2)}
so that the
S
-matrix (
119
) truncates a matrix of size
2\ell\times 2\ell
under the shortening condition (
104c
). However, the matrix has rank
\ell
and it acts non-trivially only on
\mathscr{D}_{\ell}^{(1)}
under the change (
127
) of basis

\widetilde{S}_{jj^{\prime}}:=G^{-1}S_{jj^{\prime}}G(q,t=q^{-(2\ell-1)/2})\big|_{\mathscr{D}^{(1)}_{\ell}}~,\qquad 0\leq j,j^{\prime}\leq\ell-1

(130)

where
G
is a matrix of size
2\ell\times 2\ell
that changes the basis according to (
127
). This gives the geometric interpretation of the basis change in
(
KSY 18
, §4.1)
.
As a result, we find the following explicit forms of the
S
and
T
matrices, and a 3d interpretation of our
A
-model setup in §
3.1.1
will identify an intrinsic physical meaning of the
\PSL(2,\mathbb{Z})
representation:

Conjecture 2.2
.

The space
\mathscr{D}^{(1)}_{\ell}
is an
\ell
-dimensional
\mathrm{PSL}(2,\mathbb{Z})
representation, with modular
S
and
T
matrices given by

\displaystyle T_{jj^{\prime}}\big|_{\mathscr{D}^{(1)}_{\ell}}

\displaystyle=e^{\frac{(\ell-1)\pi i}{6}}q^{-\frac{(2\ell-1)(\ell-1)}{6}}~q^{\frac{j(k-j)}{2}}\delta_{jj^{\prime}}\qquad 0\leq j,j^{\prime}\leq\ell-1

(131)

\displaystyle S_{jj^{\prime}}\big|_{\mathscr{D}^{(1)}_{\ell}}

\displaystyle=b_{\ell}^{-1}g_{j}(q,t=q^{-(2\ell-1)/2})^{-1}\widetilde{S}_{jj^{\prime}}~.

The
\mathrm{PSL}(2,\mathbb{Z})
representation comes from a modular tensor category associated to the Argyres-Douglas theory of type
(A_{1},A_{2(\ell-1)})
. These matrices coincide with those of the
(2,2\ell+1)
Virasoro minimal model at
q=e^{-2\pi i/(2\ell+1)}
.

Here we normalize (
130
) by the Macdonald norm (
121
) and

b_{\ell}=2\prod_{i=0}^{\ell-2}(q^{1/2+i}-q^{-1/2-i})

so that
S^{2}=1
. We also normalize (
95
) by
e^{(\ell-1)\pi i/6}q^{-(2\ell-1)(\ell-1)/6}
so that
(ST)^{3}=1
.

For instance, when
\ell=2
, these matrices become

T\big|_{\mathscr{D}^{(1)}_{\ell=2}}=e^{\frac{\pi i}{6}}\begin{pmatrix}q^{-\frac{1}{2}}&0\\
0&q^{\frac{1}{2}}\end{pmatrix}~,\qquad S\big|_{\mathscr{D}^{(1)}_{\ell=2}}=\frac{i}{q^{\frac{1}{2}}-q^{-\frac{1}{2}}}\begin{pmatrix}1&-(q-1+q^{-1})\\
1&-1\end{pmatrix}~.

When
q=e^{-2\pi i/5}
, they coincide with the modular matrices of the
(2,5)
Virasoro minimal model although an appropriate change of basis is required to bring the
S
-matrix into the standard form.

finite-dim irrep

shortening condition

\displaystyle A
-brane condition

\displaystyle\mathscr{F}_{m}^{(x_{m},y_{m})}

\displaystyle q^{m}=1

m=\frac{1}{\hbar}

\displaystyle\mathscr{U}_{n}

\displaystyle q^{2n}=1

n=\frac{1}{2\hbar}

\displaystyle\mathscr{V}_{k+1}

\displaystyle t^{2}=-q^{-k}

k=\frac{1}{2\hbar}+\frac{{}_{p}+i\talpha_{p}}{i\hbar}

\displaystyle\mathscr{D}_{\ell}

\displaystyle t=q^{-\ell+1/2}

\ell=\frac{{}_{p}+i\talpha_{p}}{2i\hbar}

Table 2:
A summary of finite-dimensional irreducible representations of
S\!\accentset{\mbox{\large.\hskip-1.33333pt.}}{H}
with corresponding shortening and
A
-brane conditions.

2.7
Bound states of branes and short exact sequences: morphism matching

We have hitherto studied generic conditions when an individual
A
-brane supported on a compact irreducible Lagrangian can exist. Next, we will figure out the situation in which two distinct
A
-branes are present at a singular fiber of the Hitchin fibration. When two distinct
A
-branes intersect at a singular fiber, they will form a bound state. In this section, we will study a bound state of compact
A
-branes and identify the corresponding
S\!\accentset{\mbox{\large.\hskip-1.33333pt.}}{H}
-module. This provide evidence of the equivalent morphism structure under the functor (
3
), restricting to the subcategory of compact Lagrangian
A
-branes with that of finite-dimensional
S\!\accentset{\mbox{\large.\hskip-1.33333pt.}}{H}
-modules.

2.7.1
At singular fiber of type
I_{2}

As seen in §
2.6.1
and §
2.6.2
, the compact branes
\mathfrak{B}_{\mathbf{F}}
and
\mathfrak{B}_{\mathbf{U}_{i}}
can exist when
q
is a root of unity and
t
is generic. As Figure
6
shows, the irreducible components
\mathbf{U}_{1}
and
\mathbf{U}_{2}
at the singular fiber
\pi^{-1}(b_{1})
of type
I_{2}
intersect at two points
p_{1}
and
p_{2}
. Therefore, the Floer complex
Flo 88
;
Flo 89
(or morphisms) of the two
A
-branes
\mathfrak{B}_{\mathbf{U}_{1}}
and
\mathfrak{B}_{\mathbf{U}_{2}}
is

\Hom^{*}(\mathfrak{B}_{\mathbf{U}_{1}},\mathfrak{B}_{\mathbf{U}_{2}}):=CF^{*}(\mathfrak{B}_{\mathbf{U}_{1}},\mathfrak{B}_{\mathbf{U}_{2}})\cong\mathbb{C}\langle p_{1}\rangle\oplus\mathbb{C}\langle p_{2}\rangle~.

(132)

Note that the Floer complexes
CF^{*}(\mathfrak{B}_{\mathbf{U}_{1}},\mathfrak{B}_{\mathbf{U}_{2}})
and
CF^{*}(\mathfrak{B}_{\mathbf{U}_{2}},\mathfrak{B}_{\mathbf{U}_{1}})
and the differentials on them are Poincaré-dual to each other. Namely,
each intersection point
p_{i}
defines generators of both complexes, whose degrees sum to
2
(the complex dimension of the target).

Figure 6:
At the singular fiber
\pi^{-1}(b_{1})
,
\xi_{2}
exchanges the irreducible components,
\mathbf{U}_{1}
and
\mathbf{U}_{2}
, by the
180^{\circ}
rotation along the
(0,1)
-circle (longitude). Therefore,
\xi_{2}
exchanges
p_{1}
and
p_{2}
. On the other hand,
\iota
exchanges
\mathbf{U}_{1}
and
\mathbf{U}_{2}
by fixing
p_{1}
and
p_{2}
. Besides,
\xi_{1}
maps each irreducible component to itself by the
180^{\circ}
rotation along the
(1,0)
-circle (meridian).

This implies that there are two bound states of
\mathfrak{B}_{\mathbf{U}_{1}}
and
\mathfrak{B}_{\mathbf{U}_{2}}
as
A
-branes. Let us consider one natural candidate for them: a brane
\mathfrak{B}_{\mathbf{F}}^{\lambda}
degenerating into the singular fiber
\pi^{-1}(b_{1})
of type
I_{2}
. First of all, the dimension
m
of
\Hom(\mathfrak{B}_{\text{cc}},\mathfrak{B}_{\mathbf{F}}^{\lambda})
needs to be even
m=2n
in order for the brane to be supported on a singular fiber because the evaluation of the integral cohomology class
[F^{\prime}_{\mathbf{F}}/(2\pi)]
over a singular fiber cannot be odd like (
107
). There is also a topological constraint to be a bound state of the branes
\mathfrak{B}_{\mathbf{U}_{1}}
and
\mathfrak{B}_{\mathbf{U}_{2}}
. As illustrated in Figure
6
,
a one-cycle, say the
(1,0)
-cycle, of a torus is pinched to a double point at two locations so that the singular fiber
\pi^{-1}(b_{1})
topologically consists of two copies of
\mathbb{C}\mathbf{P}^{1}
. Therefore, it has the unique bounding spin structure along the
(1,0)
-cycle, which is Neveu-Schwarz. Consequently, only a brane
\mathfrak{B}_{\mathbf{F}}^{(-,+)}
with trivial holonomy and the Neveu-Schwarz spin structure along the
(1,0)
-cycle of
\mathbf{F}
can degenerate to a bound state of the branes
\mathfrak{B}_{\mathbf{U}_{1}}
and
\mathfrak{B}_{\mathbf{U}_{2}}
at the singular fiber
\pi^{-1}(b_{1})
.

There is indeed a corresponding representation of
S\!\accentset{\mbox{\large.\hskip-1.33333pt.}}{H}
.
We see that the support
\mathbf{U}_{1}\cup\mathbf{U}_{2}
is invariant under
\tau_{-}
(as a set). Thus, a brane
\mathfrak{B}_{\mathbf{F}}^{(x_{2n},+)}
can enter the singular fiber when the corresponding module
\mathscr{F}_{2n}^{(-,+)}
is
\tau_{-}
-invariant, namely when the two ideals

(X^{2n}+X^{-2n}-x_{2n}-x_{2n}^{-1}),\qquad(\tau_{-}(X^{2n}+X^{-2n}-x_{2n}-x_{2n}^{-1}))~

coincide.
Under the condition (
104a
), the
2n
-th Macdonald polynomial takes the form
P_{2n}=X^{2n}+X^{-2n}+2=(X^{n}+X^{-n})^{2}
, and (
95
) yields
\tau_{-}(1)=1
and
\tau_{-}(P_{2n})=t^{-2n}P_{2n}
. For a generic value of
t
, only when
x_{2n}=-1
, we therefore have the
\tau_{-}
-invariant module
\mathscr{F}_{2n}^{(-,+)}\cong\mathscr{P}/(P_{2n})
.
Moreover, since
P_{2n}=(P_{n})^{2}
under (
104a
), the quotient of the polynomial representation
\mathscr{P}
yields a short exact sequence

\displaystyle 0\to\mathscr{U}_{n}^{(2)}\to\mathscr{F}_{2n}^{(-,+)}\to\mathscr{U}_{n}^{(1)}\to 0~.

(133)

The representation
\mathscr{F}_{2n}^{(-,+)}
corresponds to the bound state
\mathfrak{B}_{\mathbf{F}}^{(-,+)}
. As explained in §
2.6
, the raising operator (
85
) of
\mathscr{P}
does not become null because the prefactor
(1-q^{2j}t^{2})
cancels with the denominator of
P_{j+1}
. Consequently, this short exact sequence (
133
) does not split as a direct sum, but rather is a nontrivial extension of
\mathscr{U}_{n}^{(1)}
by
\mathscr{U}_{n}^{(2)}
. This is analogous to the fact that
\mathbb{C}[X]/(X^{2n})\to\mathbb{C}[X]/(X^{n})
cannot split as a
\mathbb{C}[X]
-module. As such, when the gradings are chosen such that
\mathscr{U}_{n}^{(1,2)}
are in degree zero, the degree of the corresponding morphism between the
A
-branes is one, and corresponds to the class in
\Hom^{1}(\mathfrak{B}_{\mathbf{U}_{1}},\mathfrak{B}_{\mathbf{U}_{2}})
represented by
\mathfrak{B}_{\mathbb{F}}^{(-,+)}
.
10
10

10

Often literature in mathematics uses the notation
\textrm{Ext}^{1}
instead of
\Hom^{1}
. Here they have the same meaning.

Although this paper does not determine the degree of the morphism in the
A
-brane category, the representation category of
S\!\accentset{\mbox{\large.\hskip-1.33333pt.}}{H}
predicts one. Even in what follows, non-trivial extensions in the representation category give a description of degree-one morphisms (extensions or bound states) of various distinct compact
A
-branes. Determining the degree of the morphisms directly in the
A
-brane category is left for future work.

Since
\Hom^{*}(\mathfrak{B}_{\mathbf{U}_{1}},\mathfrak{B}_{\mathbf{U}_{2}})
is two-dimensional, there must be another generator. To identify it, we consider the symmetries. As Figure
6
illustrates,
\xi_{2}
and
\iota
exchange the irreducible components
\mathbf{U}_{1}
and
\mathbf{U}_{2}
at the singular fiber. More precisely,
\xi_{2}
acts on the singular fiber as the
180^{\circ}
rotation along the
(0,1)
-circle (longitude) so that the intersection points
p_{1,2}
are exchanged by
\xi_{2}
. On the other hand,
\iota
exchanges
\mathbf{U}_{1}
and
\mathbf{U}_{2}
by fixing
p_{1,2}
. Consequently, the images of the brane
\mathfrak{B}_{\mathbf{F}}^{(-,+)}
under the symmetries
\xi_{2}
and
\iota
are non-isomorphic objects in the
A
-brane category. They indeed span the morphism space

\Hom^{1}(\mathfrak{B}_{\mathbf{U}_{2}},\mathfrak{B}_{\mathbf{U}_{1}})\cong\mathbb{C}\langle\xi_{2}(\mathfrak{B}_{\mathbf{F}}^{(-,+)})\rangle\oplus\mathbb{C}\langle\iota(\mathfrak{B}_{\mathbf{F}}^{(-,+)})\rangle~.

(134)

As a result, two irreducible branes can form bound states in more than one
way.
Similarly, the images of the brane
\mathscr{F}_{2n}^{(-,+)}
under the symmetries
\xi_{2}
and
\iota
are non-isomorphic in the representation category of
S\!\accentset{\mbox{\large.\hskip-1.33333pt.}}{H}
for
n>1
. The image of the short exact sequence (
133
) under
\xi_{2}
becomes

\displaystyle 0\to\mathscr{U}_{n}^{(1)}\to\xi_{2}(\mathscr{F}_{2n}^{(-,+)})\to\mathscr{U}_{n}^{(2)}\to 0~.

(135)

Likewise, The image of the short exact sequence (
133
) under
\iota
becomes

\displaystyle 0\to\mathscr{U}_{n}^{(1)}\to\iota(\mathscr{F}_{2n}^{(-,+)})\to\mathscr{U}_{n}^{(2)}\to 0~.

(136)

By using the polynomial representation (
80
), one can read off the action of the generators
x
and
y
on these representations as

\displaystyle x\Big|_{\xi_{2}(\mathscr{F}_{2n}^{(-,+)})}=\raisebox{-62.59596pt}{\includegraphics[width]{matrix}}~,\qquad x\Big|_{\iota(\mathscr{F}_{2n}^{(-,+)})}=\raisebox{-62.59596pt}{\includegraphics[width]{matrix2}}~,

(137)

on the basis where
y
acts diagonally as
\textrm{diag}(t+t^{-1},qt+q^{-1}t^{-1},\ldots,q^{2n-1}t+q^{1-2n}t^{-1})
. Note that the upper-left block and lower-right matrices of the
x
actions are the same whereas the lower-left matrices are different. These matrices explicitly show that
\xi_{2}(\mathscr{F}_{2n}^{(-,+)})
and
\iota(\mathscr{F}_{2n}^{(-,+)})
are not isomorphic.

In fact, the composition
\xi_{2}\circ\iota
leaves
\mathfrak{B}_{\mathbf{U}_{1}}
and
\mathfrak{B}_{\mathbf{U}_{2}}
as they are, respectively. However, it maps
\mathfrak{B}_{\mathbf{F}}^{(-,+)}
to a different object. Correspondingly, we have a short exact sequence

\displaystyle 0\to\mathscr{U}_{n}^{(2)}\to\xi_{2}\circ\iota(\mathscr{F}_{2n}^{(-,+)})\to\mathscr{U}_{n}^{(1)}\to 0~,

(138)

which is not isomorphic to (
133
). Therefore, they span the morphism space of two dimensions

\Hom^{1}(\mathfrak{B}_{\mathbf{U}_{1}},\mathfrak{B}_{\mathbf{U}_{2}})\cong\mathbb{C}\langle\mathfrak{B}_{\mathbf{F}}^{(-,+)}\rangle\oplus\mathbb{C}\langle\xi_{2}\circ\iota(\mathfrak{B}_{\mathbf{F}}^{(-,+)})\rangle~,

(139)

which is Poincaré-dual to (
134
). In conclusion, when two irreducible branes intersect two points, they can form non-isomorphic bound states with the same support in the
A
-brane category, and these bound states are related to subtleties defining
A
-branes supported on singular submanifolds.

At the other singular fibers
\pi^{-1}(b_{2,3})
, there are similar bound states. As in (
113
),
\sigma\in\PSL(2,\mathbb{Z})
maps (
134
) to
\Hom^{1}(\mathfrak{B}_{\mathbf{U}_{3}},\mathfrak{B}_{\mathbf{U}_{4}})
. Also,
\tau_{+}\in\PSL(2,\mathbb{Z})
maps (
134
) to
\Hom^{1}(\mathfrak{B}_{\mathbf{U}_{5}},\mathfrak{B}_{\mathbf{U}_{6}})
.

2.7.2
At global nilpotent cone of type
I_{0}^{*}

Next, let us consider the case in which both the
A
-branes
\mathfrak{B}_{\mathbf{V}}
and
\mathfrak{B}_{\mathbf{D}_{i}}
exist. In order for both
\mathfrak{B}_{\mathbf{V}}
and
\mathfrak{B}_{\mathbf{D}_{i}}
to be Lagrangian, (
114
) and (
122
) need to be satisfied, which implies
{}_{p}=0
and
\hbar
is real whereas
\talpha_{p}
and
\tbeta_{p}
can be arbitrary. Therefore, the symplectic form must be
\omega_{\mathfrak{X}}=\omega_{K}/\hbar
.
In this situation,
\mathbf{F}
and
\mathbf{U}_{i}
are also Lagrangian with respect to the symplectic form. Moreover, the quantization conditions, (
123
) and (
116
), for both
\mathfrak{B}_{\mathbf{D}_{i}}
and
\mathfrak{B}_{\mathbf{V}}
are

-c+\frac{1}{2}=\ell~,\qquad\frac{1}{2\hbar}+2c-1=k+1~,

(140)

which implies that
1/2\hbar=2\ell+k+1
. In other words, the two shortening conditions lead to the other one

\eqref{case3}\ \ \textrm{and}\ \ \iota\textrm{\eqref{case2}}\quad\longrightarrow\quad\eqref{case1}\ \ \textrm{where}\ \ n=2\ell+k+1~.

Under this condition, there are therefore finite-dimensional representations of three kinds,
\Hom(\mathfrak{B}_{\text{cc}},\mathfrak{B}_{\mathbf{U}_{i}})
,
\Hom(\mathfrak{B}_{\text{cc}},\mathfrak{B}_{\mathbf{V}})
and
\Hom(\mathfrak{B}_{\text{cc}},\mathfrak{B}_{\mathbf{D}_{i}})
. On the representation theory side, the quotient of the polynomial representation yields a short exact sequence

0\longrightarrow\iota(\mathscr{V}_{k+1})\longrightarrow\mathscr{U}_{n}^{(1)}\xrightarrow{f}\mathscr{D}_{\ell}^{(1)}\oplus\mathscr{D}_{\ell}^{(2)}\longrightarrow 0~.

(141)

We also note that there exist similar short exact sequences for the images of
\mathscr{U}_{n}^{(1)}
under the symmetry
\Xi\times\PSL(2,\mathbb{Z})
in §
2.6.2
under the same shortening condition.

In a similar fashion, if the branes
\mathfrak{B}_{\mathbf{D}_{i}}
and
\mathfrak{B}_{\mathbf{U}_{i}}
exist simultaneously, their quantization conditions guarantee the existence of
\mathfrak{B}_{\mathbf{V}}
. Also, if we assume the presence of the branes
\mathfrak{B}_{\mathbf{V}}
and
\mathfrak{B}_{\mathbf{U}_{i}}
, then the quantization condition for
\mathfrak{B}_{\mathbf{D}_{i}}
follows. In fact,
it is straightforward to check that, under the relation
n=k+1+2\ell
, we have

\displaystyle\eqref{case3}\ \ \textrm{and}\ \ \eqref{case1}

\displaystyle\longrightarrow\ \ \iota\eqref{case2}~,

(142)

\displaystyle\eqref{case1}\ \ \textrm{and}\ \ \iota\eqref{case2}

\displaystyle\longrightarrow\ \ \eqref{case3}~.

Subsequently, we have the short exact sequence (
141
).

If
\tbeta_{p}\neq 0
, the Hitchin fibration has the three singular fibers of type
I_{2}
(Figure
2
), and the Lagrangians
\mathbf{V}
and
\mathfrak{B}_{\mathbf{D}_{i}}
are not contained in a Hitchin fiber. Thus, the short exact sequence (
141
) implies that a Hamiltonian isotopy can deform the brane
\mathfrak{B}_{\mathbf{U}_{1}}
in such a way that it contains
\mathfrak{B}_{\mathbf{V}}
as subbranes. The situation becomes much more lucid when
\tbeta_{p}=0
. As
\tbeta_{p}\to 0
, the three singular fibers meet simultaneously and transform into the singular fiber of type
I_{0}^{*}
, which is the global nilpotent cone. In this process, the
A
-brane
\mathfrak{B}_{\mathbf{U}_{1}}
becomes a bound state of
\mathfrak{B}_{\mathbf{D}_{1}}
,
\mathfrak{B}_{\mathbf{D}_{2}}
and
\mathfrak{B}_{\mathbf{V}}
because of (
23
). The short exact sequence (
141
) indeed corresponds to the bound state as illustrated in Figure
7
. A similar story holds for the other branes
\mathfrak{B}_{\mathbf{U}_{i}}
and they become bound states of irreducible branes according to the relation (
23
) of the second homology group.

Figure 7:
This figure depicts the correspondence between compact supports of
(B,A,A)
-branes and finite-dimensional modules of the spherical DAHA when
\hbar=1/2n
,
\talpha_{p}/2\hbar=\ell
and
\tbeta_{p}=0={}_{p}
. Note that
n=2\ell+k+1
.

As explained above, the short exact sequence (
141
) does not split into the direct sum because the raising operator (
85
) of
\mathscr{P}
never becomes null. Geometrically, the choice of the direction of the arrows in (
141
) comes from how the support (
87
) of
\mathfrak{B}_{\mathbf{P}}
intersects with the global nilpotent cone. As explained in §
2.6.3
and §
2.6.4
, the support (
87
) of
\mathfrak{B}_{\mathbf{P}}
cuts through real one-dimensional slices of the exceptional divisors
\mathbf{D}_{1,2}
, but it does not intersect with
\mathbf{V}
. As a result, the brane
\mathfrak{B}_{\mathbf{V}}
becomes a subbrane of
\mathfrak{B}_{\U_{1}}
whereas
\mathfrak{B}_{\mathbf{D}_{1}}\oplus\mathfrak{B}_{\mathbf{D}_{2}}
becomes its quotient.

On the other hand, the
\iota
-image
\iota(\mathfrak{B}_{\mathbf{P}})
intersects with
\mathbf{V}
whereas it does not with exceptional divisors
\mathbf{D}_{i}
. Consequently, there is a short exact sequence

0\longrightarrow\mathscr{D}^{(3)}\oplus\mathscr{D}^{(4)}\longrightarrow\iota(\mathscr{U}_{n}^{(1)})\longrightarrow\iota(\mathscr{V}_{n-2\ell})\longrightarrow 0~.

(143)

Once we take
\xi_{2}
-image of this short exact sequence, we have

0\longrightarrow\mathscr{D}_{\ell}^{(1)}\oplus\mathscr{D}_{\ell}^{(2)}\xrightarrow{g}\xi_{2}\circ\iota(\mathscr{U}_{n}^{(1)})\longrightarrow\iota(\mathscr{V}_{n-2\ell})\longrightarrow 0~.

(144)

because
\iota(\mathscr{V}_{n-2\ell})
is
\xi_{2}
-invariant.

Now we are ready to compare the morphism structures of the two categories under the shortening condition
\hbar=1/2n
and
\talpha_{p}/\hbar=\ell
. As Figure
7
illustrates, the supports of branes
\mathfrak{B}_{\mathbf{V}}
and
\mathfrak{B}_{\mathbf{D}_{1}}
intersect at one point
q_{1}
so that the morphism space between them is one-dimensional:

\Hom^{1}(\mathfrak{B}_{\mathbf{D}_{1}},\mathfrak{B}_{\mathbf{V}})\cong\mathbb{C}\langle q_{1}\rangle~.

(145)

This means that there is one bound state of
\mathfrak{B}_{\mathbf{V}}
and
\mathfrak{B}_{\mathbf{D}_{1}}
. Indeed, we find the corresponding representation from (
141
):

0\longrightarrow\iota(\mathscr{V}_{k+1})\longrightarrow f^{-1}(\mathscr{D}_{\ell}^{(1)})\longrightarrow\mathscr{D}_{\ell}^{(1)}\longrightarrow 0~.

(146)

Its Poincaré dual in the representation category can be obtained from (
144
)

0\longrightarrow\mathscr{D}_{\ell}^{(1)}\longrightarrow\xi_{2}\circ\iota(\mathscr{U}_{n}^{(1)})/g(\mathscr{D}_{\ell}^{(2)})\longrightarrow\iota(\mathscr{V}_{n-2\ell})\longrightarrow 0~.

(147)

By using the sign change group
\Xi
, we obtain short exact sequences analogous to (
146
), which changes from
\mathscr{D}_{\ell}^{(1)}
to
\mathscr{D}_{\ell}^{(i)}
(
i=2,3,4
).
We can further pursue the comparison of the morphism structure. In the
A
-brane category, the morphism space between
\mathfrak{B}_{\mathbf{V}}
and
\mathfrak{B}_{\mathbf{D}_{1}}\oplus\mathfrak{B}_{\mathbf{D}_{2}}
is two-dimensional:

\Hom^{1}(\mathfrak{B}_{\mathbf{D}_{1}}\oplus\mathfrak{B}_{\mathbf{D}_{2}},\mathfrak{B}_{\mathbf{V}})\cong\mathbb{C}\langle q_{1}\rangle\oplus\mathbb{C}\langle q_{2}\rangle~.

(148)

It is easy to find the corresponding representations

\displaystyle 0\longrightarrow\iota(\mathscr{V}_{k+1})\longrightarrow f^{-1}(\mathscr{D}_{\ell}^{(1)})\oplus\mathscr{D}_{\ell}^{(2)}\longrightarrow\mathscr{D}_{\ell}^{(1)}\oplus\mathscr{D}_{\ell}^{(2)}\longrightarrow 0~,

(149)

\displaystyle 0\longrightarrow\iota(\mathscr{V}_{k+1})\longrightarrow f^{-1}(\mathscr{D}_{\ell}^{(2)})\oplus\mathscr{D}_{\ell}^{(1)}\longrightarrow\mathscr{D}_{\ell}^{(1)}\oplus\mathscr{D}_{\ell}^{(2)}\longrightarrow 0~.

In fact, the short exact sequence (
141
) can be understood as the diagonal element corresponding to
q_{1}+q_{2}\in\Hom^{1}(\mathfrak{B}_{\mathbf{D}_{1}}\oplus\mathfrak{B}_{\mathbf{D}_{2}},\mathfrak{B}_{\mathbf{V}})
. More generally, we have

\Hom^{1}(\oplus_{i\in I}\mathfrak{B}_{\mathbf{D}_{i}},\mathfrak{B}_{\mathbf{V}})\cong\oplus_{i\in I}\mathbb{C}\langle q_{i}\rangle~,

(150)

where
I
is a subset of
\{1,2,3,4\}
. The diagonal element in the representation category is

0\longrightarrow\iota(\mathscr{V}_{k+1})\longrightarrow\mathscr{N}_{|I|\ell+k+1}^{I}\longrightarrow\oplus_{i\in I}\mathscr{D}_{\ell}^{(i)}\longrightarrow 0~,

(151)

where
|I|
is the cardinality of the set
I
. We write the corresponding
A
-brane

\mathfrak{B}_{\mathbf{N}^{I}}\in\Hom^{1}(\oplus_{i\in I}\mathfrak{B}_{\mathbf{D}_{i}},\mathfrak{B}_{\mathbf{V}})~,

(152)

which is supported on
\mathbf{N}^{I}:=\cup_{i\in I}\mathbf{D}_{i}\cup\mathbf{V}
.

If the cardinality
|I|
is three, the corresponding brane is supported on
\mathbf{V}
plus three exceptional divisors, and the representation
\mathscr{N}^{I}
is not obtained by a quotient of the polynomial representation. Therefore, these are
new finite-dimensional representations
, which do not appear in the theorems of Cherednik
(
Che 05
, §2.8–9)
.

When
I=\{1,2,3,4\}
, the support of the corresponding brane is the entire global nilpotent cone
\mathbf{N}
(
20
) so that we simply write it as
\mathfrak{B}_{\mathbf{N}}
. It turns out that this brane gives rise to another interesting bound state in the
A
-brane, which we will see below.

The global nilpotent cone.

In fact, when
n-k-1
is odd (or equivalently
c\in\mathbb{Z}_{\leq 0}
), there is a short exact sequence

0\longrightarrow\iota(\mathscr{V}_{k+1})\longrightarrow\mathscr{F}_{2n}^{(+,+)}\longrightarrow\mathscr{N}_{2n-k-1}\longrightarrow 0~,

(153)

In fact, when both (
104a
) and
\iota
(
104b
) are satisfied, we have
\pol(\mathsf{L}_{2n-k-1})\cdot P_{2n-k-1}=0
. Furthermore, when
n-k-1
is odd, then
\mathscr{N}_{2n-k-1}:=\mathscr{P}/(P_{2n-k-1})
becomes an irreducible module of dimension
2n-k-1
. The short exact sequence (
153
) illustrates that the module
\mathscr{N}_{2n-k-1}
can also be obtained by the quotient
\mathscr{F}_{2n}^{(+,+)}/\iota(\mathscr{V}_{k+1})
.

When
\tbeta_{p}=0={}_{p}
, the Hitchin fibration has one singular fiber of type
I_{0}^{*}
, and the entire global nilpotent cone
\mathbf{N}
is Lagrangian with respect to
\omega_{\mathfrak{X}}
.
The short exact sequence (
153
) indeed depicts the situation where the brane
\mathfrak{B}_{\mathbf{F}}^{\lambda=(+,+)}
with the Ramond spin structures enters the global nilpotent cone. Since it has a different spin structure, the brane is
not
decomposed into each irreducible component. As a result, the brane
\mathfrak{B}_{\mathbf{F}}^{\lambda=(+,+)}
becomes the bound state of two branes;
\mathfrak{B}_{\mathbf{V}}
and
\mathfrak{B}_{\mathbf{N}}
.
Actually, using the fiber class relation (
21
) with (
106
) and (
116
), one can evaluate the dimension formula for an
A
-brane
\mathfrak{B}_{\mathbf{N}}

\displaystyle\dim\Hom(\mathfrak{B}_{\text{cc}},\mathfrak{B}_{\mathbf{N}})=\int_{\mathbf{N}}\frac{F+B}{2\pi}=\int_{\mathbf{N}}\frac{\omega_{I}}{2\pi\hbar}=\frac{1}{2\hbar}-2c+1~.

(154)

From (
140
), this is equal to
2n-k-1
, and the space of
(\mathfrak{B}_{\text{cc}},\mathfrak{B}_{\mathbf{N}})
-strings therefore corresponds to the module
\mathscr{N}_{2n-k-1}
in (
153
).

One delicate point arises for constructing the Chan-Paton bundle for
\mathfrak{B}_{\mathbf{N}}
because
\mathbf{N}
is
not
a manifold. Since
\mathbf{V}
is linked with the exceptional divisors
\mathbf{D}_{i}
in
\mathfrak{B}_{\mathbf{N}}
, the Chan-Paton bundle for
\mathfrak{B}_{\mathbf{N}}
is no longer well-defined at the four joining points of
\mathbf{V}
and
\mathbf{D}_{i}
. The Chan-Paton bundle becomes a putative “line bundle”
\mathcal{L}^{\prime}
over each exceptional divisor
\mathbf{D}_{i}
and the curvature
F^{\prime}_{\mathbf{N}}
of its connection has a half-integral flux over it
FW 99

\int_{\mathbf{D}_{i}}F^{\prime}_{\mathbf{N}}=-\frac{1}{2}~,

while it cancels with the
B
-field due to (
75
)

F^{\prime}_{\mathbf{N}}+B\big|_{\mathbf{N}}=0~.

In other words,
\mathcal{L}^{\prime}
restricted to an exceptional divisor
\mathbf{D}_{i}
is a “square root” of the
\mathcal{O}(-1)\to\mathbb{C}\mathbf{P}^{1}
bundle and the
B
-field flux over it is
1/2
.
As a result, we have

\int_{\mathbf{D}_{i}}\frac{F+B}{2\pi}=\int_{\mathbf{D}_{i}}\frac{\omega_{I}}{2\pi\hbar}=\frac{n-k-1}{2}\in\frac{1}{2}+\mathbb{Z}~,

which gives the condition that
n-k-1
is odd.

Under this circumstance, the line bundle
\mathcal{L}\to\mathfrak{X}
(
61
) for
\mathfrak{B}_{\text{cc}}
is actually the
2n
-th tensor product of the determinant line bundle
(
HL 10
, §8)
of the Hitchin moduli space. As a result, the geometric quantization of
\mathbf{V}
provides the quantum Hilbert space
\mathscr{V}_{k+1}
on a once-punctured torus in Chern-Simons theory
Guk 11
. The additional series
\mathscr{V}_{k+1}
at a primitive
2n
-th root of unity
q=e^{\pi i/n}
is called
perfect representation

(
Che 05
, §2.9.3)
.
Moreover, when
n=k+2
, the additional series
\mathscr{V}_{k+1}
of dimension
k+1
is isomorphic to the well-known Verlinde formula of
\widehat{\mathfrak{sl}}(2)_{k}
with level
k
for a torus (without puncture)
Ver 88
.

Let us end this section by commenting briefly on future directions.
There are an enormous number of non-compact Lagrangian submanifolds in the moduli space of Higgs bundles that have been studied in their own right: for example, the image of the Hitchin section, the brane of opers (see
NW 10
;
Mik 18
;
BT 18
;
GW21a
in a similar context), or the
A
-polynomial of any knot
Guk 05
. Each of these geometric objects should naturally be associated with an
S\!\accentset{\mbox{\large.\hskip-1.33333pt.}}{H}
-module whose behavior precisely matches the geometric properties of the object, just as we demonstrate for compact Lagrangians and for the (generalized) polynomial representation. It would be of great interest to further pursue this correspondence for infinite-dimensional representations, even just in the rank-one case.

It would also be interesting to connect explicitly with other mathematical contexts in which algebraic approaches to the Fukaya category or equivalences between Fukaya categories and module categories appear. To give just one example, in
EL 19
, Etgü and Lekili study the Chekanov-Eliashberg dg-algebra associated with a Legendrian link in a Weinstein four-manifold for a given graph. They show that this algebra is
A_{\infty}
-quasi-isomorphic to, roughly speaking, the endomorphism algebra of a collection of generating objects of the wrapped Fukaya category of the surface, and go on to recover the multiplicative preprojective algebra studied in
CBS 06
in the context of the Deligne–Simpson problem from the Legendrian link. When the graph in question is the affine
D_{4}
Dynkin diagram, it is expected that the corresponding preprojective algebra is related to DAHA. (We thank A. Oblomkov for private communication related to this point.) The computations of the (wrapped) Fukaya category of the above four-manifolds in
EL 19
thus may provide an interesting perspective on our Claim
1.1
as well as its generalization to other algebras.

3
3d theories and modularity

In this section, we connect the brane setup of the above 2d
A
-model to 3d/3d correspondence and shed light on various modular representations coming from geometry. In particular, we explain the origin for the explicit form of the
S
and
T
matrices in Conjectures
2.1
and
2.2
.
The modular action in Conjecture
2.1
turns out to be the one of refined Chern-Simons theory
AS 15
.
On the other hand, the modular action in Conjecture
2.2
is a “hidden” (surprising) one;
it is realized on the vector space spanned by the set of connected components of fixed points under the Hitchin
\U(1)_{\beta}
action on the moduli space of wild Higgs bundles associated to a certain Argyres-Douglas theory.
Furthermore, we propose how non-standard (
e.g.
logarithmic) modular data of
\MTC[M_{3}]
can be described in terms of the
A
-model on the Hitchin moduli space associated with the Heegaard decomposition of
M_{3}
and discuss possible connections to skein modules of closed oriented 3-manifolds.

One advantage of connecting the 2d
A
-model to the three-dimensional perspective is that all of these modular actions admit a natural categorification. In other words, in all of these instances it makes sense to ask if the space of open strings in the Hitchin moduli space can be realized as the Grothendieck groups of a tensor category (possibly, non-unitary or non-semisimple):

\SL(2,\mathbb{Z})\ \rotatebox[origin]{-90.0}{$\circlearrowright$}\ K^{0}(\MTC)~.

Finally, we will see that, in the opposite direction, the relation to the 2d
A
-model offers a unifying home for the above-mentioned modular data.

3.1
DAHA and modularity

The fivebrane system in M-theory that provides geometric origins of the modular representations on DAHA modules is the following familiar setting for the 3d/3d correspondence

\begin{matrix}{\mbox{\rm space-time:}}&\quad&S^{1}&\times_{q,t}&\big(TN&\times&T^{*}M_{3}\big)\\
{\mbox{\rm$N$ M5-branes:}}&\quad&S^{1}&\times_{q}&D^{2}&\times&M_{3}\end{matrix}

(155)

where
M_{3}
is a 3-manifold,
D^{2}
is a two-dimensional disk (or a cigar), and
TN\cong\mathbb{R}^{4}
is the Taub-NUT space.
Writing the local complex coordinates
(z_{1},z_{2})
on
TN
, such that
z_{1}
also parametrizes
D^{2}
, we turn on the
\Omega
-background,
i.e.
a holonomy along
S^{1}
that provides a twisting of
TN
via an isometry

(z_{1},z_{2})\to(qz_{1},t^{-1}z_{2})~.

(156)

In this setting, the symmetry group of the 6d
(2,0)
theory on the M5-branes is reduced to

\SO(6)_{E}\times\SO(5)_{R}\to\SO(3)_{1}\times\SO(3)_{2}\times\SO(3)_{R}\times\SO(2)_{R}~,

(157)

where
\SO(3)_{1}
and
\SO(3)_{2}
are the space-time symmetry of
S^{1}\times_{q}D^{2}
and
M_{3}
, respectively, and
\SO(3)_{R}
is the symmetry of a cotangent fiber of
T^{*}M_{3}
. We perform a topological twist by taking the diagonal subgroup
\SO(3)_{\textrm{diag}}
of
\SO(3)_{2}\times\SO(3)_{R}
so that the resulting theory is partially topological (along
M_{3}
). After the partial topological twist, the effective theory on
S^{1}\times_{q}D^{2}
only depends on topology (but not the metric) on
M_{3}
and is described by 3d
\mathcal{N}=2
theory often denoted
\mathcal{T}[M_{3}]

11
11

11

In this section, we restrict ourselves to
\SU(N)
gauge group so that
\mathcal{T}[M_{3},\SU(N)]=\mathcal{T}[M_{3}]
.
, with the
R
-symmetry given by
\SO(2)_{R}
in (
157
). When
M_{3}
is a Seifert manifold, there is an extra
\U(1)_{S}
symmetry associated with the two directions in the cotangent bundle normal to the Seifert fiber. As a result, the partition function, called the half-index, of the 3d
\mathcal{N}=2
theory
\mathcal{T}[M_{3}]
on
S^{1}\times_{q}D^{2}
with a 2d
\mathcal{N}=(0,2)
boundary condition
\mathcal{B}
in this setting is defined as

Z_{\mathcal{T}[M_{3}]}(S^{1}\times_{q}D^{2},\mathcal{B})=\Tr(-1)^{F}e^{-\beta(\Delta-R-J_{3}/2)}q^{J_{3}+S}t^{R-S}~,

(158)

where
S
and
R
are charges of
\U(1)_{S}
and
\SO(2)_{R}
, respectively,
\Delta
is the Hamiltonian, and
J_{3}
is an eigenvalue of the Cartan subalgebra of
\SO(3)_{1}
. The difference between
\U(1)_{S}
and
\SO(2)_{R}
is customarily denoted
\U(1)_{\beta}
in
GP 15
;
GPV 17
;
GPPV 20
, and its fugacity is the variable
t
in (
158
).

Notice that the system (
155
) does not involve a once-punctured torus which was used to define the Hitchin moduli space and the parameter
t
as in the previous section. However, for gauge groups of type
A
, the following two physical systems are expected to be closely related:

•

6d
(2,0)
theory on
S^{1}\times C_{p}
with
C_{p}
being a once-punctured torus.

•

4d
\mathcal{N}=2^{*}
theory on
S^{1}
.

Although the two systems would have different spectra,
12
12

12

For example, many KK modes of the 6d theory on
T^{2}
have no counterparts in the 4d theory. Even if one replaces the 4d
\mathcal{N}=2^{*}
theory with 6d
(2,0)
theory on a torus (with the mass parameter replaced by holonomies for a
\U(1)
subgroup of the R-symmetry on
T^{2}
) the full spectrum is still different. One way to see this is that the latter theory depends on all three
\U(1)
holonomies on
T^{2}\times S^{1}
in a periodic way, and they are completely symmetric, while this is not the case for the former theory obtained from a punctured torus.
their BPS sectors are expected to be equivalent. In particular, at low energy both systems realize a 3d sigma-model onto the Hitchin moduli space. The deformation parameters can also be identified as follows.

On one side, the (classical) deformations are parametrized by the triplet
(\alpha_{p},\beta_{p},\gamma_{p})
of monodromy parameters around the puncture as introduced before.
On the other side, for the 4d
\mathcal{N}=2^{*}
theory, the triplet of deformation parameters is given by the complex mass of the adjoint hyper-multiplet in 4d together with the holonomy of the
\U(1)
flavor symmetry along the circle. In the system (
155
), the 4d
\mathcal{N}=2^{*}
theory is obtained by the compactification of the 6d theory on
T^{2}\subset M_{3}
with holonomy for
\U(1)_{\beta}
along
S^{1}
(
158
). In particular, the parameter
t
defined above is identified with the
t
in DAHA. In this section, we will be looking at questions whose answers depend holomorphically on
t
, as required by supersymmetry on
M_{3}
, and the other deformation parameter
\beta_{p}
won’t play a role. For example, what complex connections on
T^{2}\subset M_{3}
can be extended to the entire
M_{3}
is a question that is “holomorphic in
J
” (and given by intersections of
(A,B,A)
-branes in the Hitchin moduli space). Notice that this non-trivial relation only holds for a gauge group of type
A
, while for other types the class
\mathcal{S}
construction of 4d
\mathcal{N}=2^{*}
theory is generally unknown, and the once-punctured torus does not lead to either the 4d
\mathcal{N}=2^{*}
theory or DAHA.

One statement of the 3d/3d correspondence is the duality between the
non-perturbative
complex
\SL(N,\mathbb{C})
Chern-Simons theory on
M_{3}
and the 3d
\mathcal{N}=2
theory
\mathcal{T}[M_{3}]
on
S^{1}\times_{q}D^{2}
, so that the partition functions of both sides are identified. As explained in
GPV 17
;
GPPV 20
;
GM 19
, for a particular class of boundary conditions
\mathcal{B}_{b}
labeled by
b\in(\Spin^{c}(M_{3}))^{N-1}
, the partition function of the 3d
\mathcal{N}=2
theory
\mathcal{T}[M_{3}]
on
S^{1}\times_{q}D^{2}
counts BPS states and, therefore, has a
q
-expansions with integer coefficients and integer
q
-powers
13
13

13

Up to an overall factor
q^{\Delta_{b}}
that plays an important role but is not relevant to the present discussion.

\widehat{Z}_{\mathcal{T}[M_{3}],b}(q,t):=Z_{\mathcal{T}[M_{3}]}(S^{1}\times_{q}D^{2},\mathcal{B}_{b})~.

(159)

The relation to Chern-Simons theory involves the same space of boundary conditions with a “dual” basis, related to
\mathcal{B}_{b}
via the
\mathbb{S}
-matrix

\mathbb{S}_{ab}=\frac{\sum_{\sigma\in\mathfrak{S}_{N}}e^{2\pi i\sum_{i=1}^{N-1}{\ell k}(a_{i},b_{\sigma(i)})}}{|\mathrm{Stab}_{\mathfrak{S}_{N}}(a)|\cdot|{\mathrm{Tor}\,}H_{1}(M_{3},\mathbb{Z})|^{(N-1)/2}}~.

In particular, the partition function of the non-perturbative
\SL(N,\mathbb{C})
Chern-Simons theory on
M_{3}
is given by

\Big(-\frac{\log q}{4\pi i}\Big)^{\frac{N-1}{4}}\sum_{a,b\in(\Spin^{c}(M_{3}))^{N-1}}e^{2\pi i\bar{k}\cdot{\ell k}(a,a)}\mathbb{S}_{ab}\widehat{Z}_{\mathcal{T}[M_{3}],b}(q,t)

(160)

with generic

, and specializes to that of
\SU(N)
Chern-Simons theory when

with integer (renormalized) level
\bar{k}=k+N
. The origin of
\log q
factors is explained in
PY 16
.
Note that the linking pairing
{\ell k}
on
\Spin^{c}(M_{3})
is defined by the Pontryagin duality.
We will see shortly that it is the basis of BPS partition functions (
159
) and the corresponding boundary conditions
\mathcal{B}_{b}
that are most naturally related to DAHA.

Consider a simple example where
M_{3}=L(p,1)
is a Lens space. The lens space
L(p,1)
can be constructed by gluing two solid tori with a homeomorphism between the boundary tori sending the meridian
(1,0)
of one torus to a
(1,p)
cycle of the other.
The corresponding 3d
\mathcal{N}=2

\SU(N)
gauge theory
\mathcal{T}[L(p,1)]
consists of one adjoint chiral multiplet
\Phi
with
R

\mathcal{N}=2

.
Consequently, the factor
\widehat{Z}_{\mathcal{T}[L(p,1)],b}
labeled by

is defined by

\widehat{Z}_{\mathcal{T}[L(p,1)],b}(q,t)={\frac{1}{N!}}\int_{|X|=1}\frac{dX}{2\pi iX}\;\Upsilon(X;q,t)\Theta_{b}^{\mathbb{Z}^{N-1};p}(X,q)~,

(161)

where

\Upsilon(X)=\prod_{\alpha\in\mathsf{R}}\frac{(X^{\alpha};q^{2})_{\infty}}{(t^{2}X^{\alpha};q^{2})_{\infty}}~,\qquad\Theta_{b}^{\mathbb{Z}^{N-1};p}(X,q)=\sum_{n\in p\mathbb{Z}^{N-1}+b}q^{2\sum_{i=1}^{N-1}{n_{i}^{2}}/p}\prod_{i=1}^{N-1}X_{i}^{n_{i}}~.

Here we impose the Neumann boundary condition at the boundary
\partial(S^{1}\times_{q}D^{2})
on the vector multiplet and adjoint chiral multiplet, which give rise to the numerator and denominator of the Macdonald measure
\Upsilon
by one-loop determinant
YS 20
(see also (
222

\Theta_{b}^{\mathbb{Z}^{N-1};p}
encodes the information about the Chern-Simons term with level
p
, and 2d
\mathcal{N}=(0,2)
boundary condition at the boundary

is labeled by

. In fact,

can be understood as the half-index of the 3d/2d coupled system. For more detail, we refer to

;
GPPV 20
.

When the lens space

is constructed by gluing two solid tori, we can include a Wilson loop in each solid torus. The

In particular, when

One important lesson that we learn in this subsection is that the Hilbert space of a non-perturbative complex Chern-Simons TQFT on a 2-torus is the space of representations of the spherical DAHA at
t=1
. A categorified version of this statement would be a relation between the category of line operators in the
\widehat{Z}
TQFT and the category of modules of
S\!\accentset{\mbox{\large.\hskip-1.33333pt.}}{H}_{t=1}
,

\MTC(\widehat{Z})\cong\Rep(S\!\accentset{\mbox{\large.\hskip-1.33333pt.}}{H}_{t=1})~.

(168)

Again, we remind that here and in other places,
\MTC
refers to a tensor category where some of the traditional conditions may need to be relaxed,
e.g.
it may have an infinite number of simple objects, be non-unitary or non-semisimple. (The latter generalization typically appears when one tries to “truncate” a category with infinitely many simple objects to a finite-dimensional structure.) The modular representations that arise from such generalizations are, in general, more delicate and interesting than familiar vector-valued modular forms that describe the space of genus-1 conformal blocks in a rational VOA. Of course, in some special cases, these more interesting and exotic generalizations do not arise, and
\MTC
is a genuine modular tensor category in its full mathematical sense (justifying the name for generalizations as well); this happens in some of the examples discussed in the following subsections and also in various examples considered in
GPV 17
;
FG 20
;
DGN+ 20
.

3.1.1
\SU(2)
: refined Chern-Simons and TQFT associated to Argyres-Douglas theory

This connection of 3d theories to the 2d sigma-model clarifies the geometric origin of the modular action.
It was proposed in

that the fivebrane system (

when the parameters are subject to

The parameters

q=\exp\Bigl(\frac{\pi i}{k+2c}\Bigr)~,\qquad t=\exp\Bigl(\frac{c\,\pi i}{k+2c}\Bigr)~.

(169)

This condition is equivalent to the existence (

The projective action of

Thus, the gluing of the two states

The

.

Although the parameters

and

are subject to

, there is one free parameter left.
If

is generic, refined Chern-Simons theory cannot arise from a fusion category due to Ocneanu rigidity (for instance, see

\MTC
).

If we further impose the condition that

In addition, the half index in (
